<span class="atom-split" data-atom="cymbeline" data-title="CYMBELINE" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro"></span>


[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
OUR first reference to [[Cymbeline]] is a note by the astrologer Simon Forman that he saw the play, probably not long before his [[Death|death]] on 8 September 1611. He refers to the heroine as ‘[[Innogen]]’, and this name occurs in the sources; the form ‘Imogen’, found only in the Folio, appears to be a misprint. The play’s courtly tone, and the [[Masque|masque]]-like quality of, particularly, the episode (5.5.186.1-2) in which Jupiter ‘descends in thunder and [[Lightning|lightning]], sitting upon an eagle’ and ‘throws a thunderbolt’, suggests that as Shakespeare wrote he may have had in mind the audiences and the stage equipment of the Blackfriars theatre, which his company used from the [[Autumn|autumn]] of 1609; and stylistic evidence places the play in about 1610-11. It was first printed in the 1623 Folio, as the last of the tragedies. In fact it is a [[Tragicomedy|tragicomedy]], or a [[Romance|romance]], telling a complex and implausible tale of events which cause the deaths of certain subsidiary characters ([[Cloten]], and the [[Queen]]) and bring major characters (including the heroine, [[Innogen]]) close to [[Death|death]], but which are miraculously resolved in the reunions and reconciliations of the closing scene.
Shakespeare’s plot reflects a wide range of reading. He took his title and setting from the name and reign of the legendary British [[King|king]] [[Cymbeline]], or Cunobelinus, said to have reigned from 33 BC till shortly after the birth of [[Christ]]. [[Cymbeline]] is no chronicle [[history]], but Shakespeare derived some ideas, and many of his characters’ names, from accounts of early British [[history]] in Holinshed’s Chronicles and elsewhere. Drawing partially, it seems, on an old play, The Rare Triumphs of [[Love]] and Fortune (acted 1582, printed 1589), he gives [[Cymbeline]] a daughter, [[Innogen]], and a wicked second [[Queen]] with a loutish, vicious son, [[Cloten]], whom she wishes to see on the throne in her husband’s place. [[Cymbeline]], disapproving of his daughter’s [[Marriage|marriage]] to ‘a poor but worthy gentleman’, [[Posthumus]] Leonatus, banishes him. The strand of plot showing the outcome of a wager that [[Posthumus]], in Rome, lays on his wife’s chastity is indebted, directly or indirectly, to Boccaccio’s Decameron. Another old play, Sir Clyomon and Clamydes (printed in 1599), may have suggested the bizarre scene (4.2) in which [[Innogen]] mistakes [[Cloten]]’s headless [[Body|body]] for that of [[Posthumus]]; and IIolinshed’s Ilistory of Scotland supplied the episode in which [[Cymbeline]]’s two sons, [[Guiderius]] and [[Arviragus]], helped only by the old man ([[Belarius]]) who has brought them up in the wilds of Wales, defeat the entire Roman army.
The tone of [[Cymbeline]] has puzzled commentators. Its prose and verse style is frequently ornate, sometimes [[Grotesque|grotesque]]. Its characterization often seems deliberately artificial. Extremes are violently juxtaposed, most daringly when [[Innogen]], supposed dead, is laid beside [[Cloten]]’s headless [[Body|body]]: the [[Beauty|beauty]] of the verse in which she is mourned, and of the [[Flowers|flowers]] strewn over the bodies, contrasts with the hideous spectacle of the headless corpse; her waking speech is one of Shakespeare’s most thrillingly difficult challenges to his performers. The appearance of Jupiter lifts the action to a new level of even greater implausibility, preparing us for the extraordinary series of revelations by which the play advances to its impossibly happy ending. [[Cymbeline]] has been valued mostly for its portrayal of [[Innogen]], ideal of womanhood to, especially, Victorian readers and theatre-goers. The play as a whole is a fantasy, an experimental exercise in virtuosity.
THE PERSONS OF THE PLAY
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]], [[King]] of Britain
Princess [[Innogen|INNOGEN]], his daughter, later disguised as a man named Fidele
[[Queen|QUEEN]], [[Cymbeline]]’s wife, [[Innogen]]’s stepmother
Lord [[Cloten|CLOTEN]], her son

[[Belarius|BELARIUS]], a banished lord, calling himself Morgan
CORNELIUS, a physician
[[Helen|HELEN]], a lady attending on [[Innogen]]
Two LORDS attending on [[Cloten]]
Two GENTLEMEN
Two British CAPTAINS
Two JAILERS
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]] Leonatus, a poor gentleman, [[Innogen]]’s husband
PISANIO, his servant
FILARIO, a friend of [[Posthumus]]
Caius [[Lucius|LUCIUS]], ambassador from Rome, later General of the Roman forces
Two Roman SENATORS
Roman TRIBUNES
A Roman CAPTAIN
Philharmonus, a SOOTHSAYER
JUPITER
[[Ghost]] of SICILIUS Leonatus, father of [[Posthumus]]
[[Ghost]] of the MOTHER of [[Posthumus]]
[[Ghosts]] of the BROTHERS of [[Posthumus]]

Lords attending on [[Cymbeline]], ladies attending on the [[Queen]], musicians attending on [[Cloten]], messengers, soldiers

[[Cymbeline]], [[King]] of Britain

1.1 Enter two Gentlemen
FIRST GENTLEMAN
You do not meet a man but frowns. Our bloods
No more obey the heavens than our courtiers
Still seem as does the [[King]].
SECOND GENTLEMAN
But what’s the matter?
FIRST GENTLEMAN
His daughter, and the heir of ’s kingdom, whom
He purposed to his wife’s sole son—a widow
That late he married—hath referred herself
Unto a poor but worthy gentleman. She’s wedded,
Her husband banished, she imprisoned. All
Is outward sorrow, though I think the [[King]]
Be touched at very heart.
SECOND GENTLEMAN
None but the [[King]]?
FIRST GENTLEMAN
He that hath lost her, too. So is the [[Queen]],
That most desired the match. But not a courtier—
Although they wear their faces to the bent
Of the [[King]]’s looks—hath a heart that is not
Glad of the thing they scowl at.
SECOND GENTLEMAN
And why so?
FIRST GENTLEMAN
He that hath missed the Princess is a thing
Too bad for bad report, and he that hath her—
I mean that married her—alack, good man,
And therefore banished!—is a creature such
As, to seek through the regions of the earth
For one his like, there would be something failing
In him that should compare. I do not think
So fair an outward and such stuff within
Endows a man but he.
SECOND GENTLEMAN
You speak him far.
FIRST GENTLEMAN
I do extend him, sir, within himself;
Crush him together rather than unfold
His measure duly.
SECOND GENTLEMAN What’s his name and birth?
FIRST GENTLEMAN
I cannot delve him to the root. His father
Was called Sicilius, who did join his [[Honour|honour]]
Against the Romans with Cassibelan
But had his titles by Tenantius, whom
He served with glory and admired [[success]],
So gained the sur-addition ‘Leonatus’;
And had, besides this gentleman in question,
Two other sons who in the wars o‘th’ [[Time|time]]
Died with their swords in hand; for which their father,
Then old and fond of issue, took such sorrow
That he quit being, and his gentle lady,
Big of this gentleman, our theme, deceased
As he was born. The [[King]], he takes the babe
To his protection, calls him [[Posthumus]] Leonatus,
Breeds him, and makes him of his bedchamber;
Puts to him all the learnings that his [[Time|time]]
Could make him the receiver of, which he took
As we do air, fast as ’twas ministered,
And in ’s [[spring]] became a harvest; lived in [[Court|court]]—
Which rare it is to do—most praised, most loved;
A sample to the youngest, to th’ more mature
A glass that feated them, and to the graver
A [[Child|child]] that guided dotards. To his mistress,
For whom he now is banished, her own price
Proclaims how she esteemed him and his virtue.
By her election may be truly read
What kind of man he is.
SECOND GENTLEMAN
I [[Honour|honour]] him
Even out of your report. But pray you tell me,
Is she sole [[Child|child]] to th’ [[King]]?
FIRST GENTLEMAN His only [[Child|child]].
He had two sons—if this be worth your hearing,
Mark it: the eld‘st of them at three years old,
I’th’ swathing clothes the other, from their nursery
Were stol’n, and to this hour no guess in knowledge
Which way they went.
SECOND GENTLEMAN How long is this ago?
FIRST GENTLEMAN Some twenty years.
SECOND GENTLEMAN
That a [[King|king]]’s children should be so conveyed,
So slackly guarded, and the search so slow
That could not trace them!
FIRST GENTLEMAN
Howsoe‘er ’tis strange,
Or that the negligence may [[Well|well]] be laughed at,
Yet is it true, sir.
SECOND GENTLEMAN I do [[Well|well]] believe you.
Enter the [[Queen]], [[Posthumus]], and [[Innogen]]
FIRST GENTLEMAN
We must forbear. Here comes the gentleman,
The [[Queen]] and Princess.
Exeunt the two Gentlemen
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
No, be assured you shall not find me, daughter,
After the slander of most stepmothers,
Evil-eyed unto you. You’re my prisoner, but
Your jailer shall deliver you the keys
That lock up your restraint. For you, [[Posthumus]],
So soon as I can win th‘offended [[King]]
I will be known your advocate. Marry, yet
The [[Fire|fire]] of rage is in him, and ’twere good
You leaned unto his sentence with what patience
Your wisdom may inform you.
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
Please your highness,
I will from hence today.
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
You know the peril.
I’ll fetch a turn about the [[Garden|garden]], pitying
The pangs of barred affections, though the [[King]]
Hath charged you should not speak together. Exit
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
O dissembling courtesy! How fine this [[Tyrant|tyrant]]
Can tickle where she wounds! My dearest husband,
I something [[Fear|fear]] my father’s wrath, but nothing—
Always reserved my holy duty—what
His rage can do on me. You must be gone,
And I shall here abide the hourly shot
Of angry eyes, not comforted to live
But that there is this [[Jewel|jewel]] in the world
That I may see again.
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
My [[Queen|queen]], my mistress!
O lady, weep no more, lest I give cause
To be suspected of more tenderness
Than doth become a man. I will remain
The loyal‘st husband that did e’er plight troth;
My residence in Rome at one Filario’s,
Who to my father was a friend, to me
Known but by letter; thither write, my [[Queen|queen]],
And with mine eyes I’ll drink the words you send
Though ink be made of gall.
Enter [[Queen]]
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
Be brief, I pray you.
If the [[King]] come, I shall incur I know not
How much of his displeasure. (Aside) Yet I’ll move him
To walk this way. I never do him wrong
But he does buy my injuries, to be friends,
Pays dear for my offences. Exit
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
Should we be taking leave
As long a term as yet we have to live,
The loathness to depart would grow. Adieu.
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]] Nay, stay a little.
Were you but riding forth to air yourself
Such parting were too petty. Look here, [[Love|love]]:
This diamond was my mother’s. Take it, heart;
She gives him a ring
But keep it till you woo another wife
When [[Innogen]] is dead.
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
How, how? Another?
You gentle gods, give me but this I have,
And cere up my embracements from a next
With bonds of [[Death|death]]! Remain, remain thou here
He puts on the ring
While sense can keep it on; and, sweetest, fairest,
As I my poor [[Self|self]] did exchange for you
To your so infinite loss, so in our trifles
I still win of you. For my sake wear this.
He gives her a bracelet
It is a manacle of [[Love|love]]. I’ll place it
Upon this fairest prisoner.
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]] O the gods!
When shall we see again?
Enter [[Cymbeline]] and lords
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
Alack, the [[King]]!
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
Thou basest thing, avoid hence, from my sight!
If after this command thou fraught the [[Court|court]]
With thy unworthiness, thou diest. Away.
Thou’rt [[Poison|poison]] to my [[Blood|blood]].
[[Posthumus|POSTHUMUS]]
The gods protect you,
And bless the good remainders of the [[Court|court]]!
I am gone.
Exit
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
There cannot be a pinch in [[Death|death]]
More sharp than this is.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
O disloyal thing,
That shouldst repair my youth, thou heap’st
A year’s age on me.
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
I beseech you, sir,
Harm not yourself with your vexation.
I am senseless of your wrath. A touch more rare
Subdues all pangs, all fears.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
Past grace, obedience-
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
Past [[Hope|hope]] and in [[Despair|despair]]: that way past grace.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
That mightst have had the sole son of my [[Queen|queen]]!
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
O blessed that I might not! I chose an eagle
And did avoid a puttock.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
Thou took’st a beggar, wouldst have made my throne
A seat for baseness.
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
No, I rather added
A lustre to it.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
O thou vile one!
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
Sir,
It is your fault that I have loved [[Posthumus]].
You bred him as my playfellow, and he is
A man worth any woman, over-buys me
Almost the sum he pays.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
What, art thou mad?
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
Almost, sir. Heaven restore me! Would I were
A neatherd’s daughter, and my Leonatus
Our neighbour shepherd’s son.
Enter [[Queen]]
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
Thou foolish thing.
(To [[Queen]]) They were again together; you have done
Not after our command. (To lords) Away with her,
And pen her up.
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
Beseech your patience, peace,
Dear lady daughter, peace. Sweet sovereign,
Leave us to ourselves, and make yourself some comfort
Out of your best advice.
[[Cymbeline|CYMBELINE]]
Nay, let her languish
A drop of [[Blood|blood]] a day, and, being aged,
Die of this folly.
Exit with lords
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
Fie, you must give way.
Enter Pisanio
Here is your servant. How now, sir? What news?
PISANIO
My lord your son drew on my master.
[[Queen|QUEEN]] Ha!
No harm, I trust, is done?
PISANIO
There might have been,
But that my master rather played than fought,
And had no help of anger. They were parted
By gentlemen at hand.
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
I am very glad on’t.
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
Your son’s my father’s friend; he takes his part
To draw upon an [[Exile|exile]]—O brave sir!
I would they were in Afric both together,
Myself by with a needle, that I might prick
The goer-back. (To Pisanio) Why came you from your
master?
PISANIO
On his command. He would not suffer me
To bring him to the haven, left these notes
Of what commands I should be subject to
When’t pleased you to employ me.
[[Queen|QUEEN]]
This hath been
Your faithful servant. I dare lay mine [[Honour|honour]]
He will remain so.
PISANIO I humbly thank your highness.
[[Queen|QUEEN]] Pray walk a while.
⌈Exit⌉
[[Innogen|INNOGEN]]
About some half hour hence, pray you speak with me.
You shall at least go see my lord aboard.
For this [[Time|time]] leave me.
Exeunt severally

### Atto 1, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Cloten]] and two Lords |
| FIRST LORD | Sir, I would advise you to shift a shirt. The violence of action hath made you reek as a sacrifice. Where air comes out, air comes in. There’s none abroad so wholesome as that you vent. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | If my shirt were bloody, then to shift it. Have I hurt him? |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) No, [[Faith\|faith]], not so much as his patience. |
| FIRST LORD | Hurt him? His [[Body\|body]]’s a passable carcass if he be not hurt. It is a thoroughfare for steel if he be not hurt. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) His steel was in debt—it went o’th’ backside the town. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | The [[Villain\|villain]] would not stand me. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) No, but he fled forward still, toward your face. |
| FIRST LORD | Stand you? You have land enough of your own, but he added to your having, gave you some ground. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) As many inches as you have oceans. Puppies! |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I would they had not come between us. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) So would I, till you had measured how long a [[Fool\|fool]] you were upon the ground. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | And that she should [[Love\|love]] this fellow and refuse me! |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) If it be a sin to make a true election, she is damned. |
| FIRST LORD | Sir, as I told you always, her [[Beauty\|beauty]] and her brain go not together. She’s a good sign, but I have seen small reflection of her wit. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) She shines not upon fools lest the reflection should hurt her. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Come, I’ll to my chamber. Would there had been some hurt done. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) I wish not so, unless it had been the fall of an ass, which is no great hurt. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | (to Second Lord) You’ll go with us? |
| FIRST LORD | I’ll attend your lordship. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Nay, come, let’s go together. |
| SECOND LORD | [[Well]], my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 1, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Innogen]] and Pisanio |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I would thou grew‘st unto the shores o’th’ haven<br>And questionedst every sail. If he should write<br>And I not have it, ’twere a paper lost<br>As offered [[Mercy\|mercy]] is. What was the last<br>That he spake to thee? |
| PISANIO | It was his [[Queen\|queen]], his [[Queen\|queen]]. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Then waved his handkerchief? |
| PISANIO | And kissed it, madam. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Senseless linen, happier therein than I!<br>And that was all? |
| PISANIO | No, madam. For so long<br>As he could make me with this eye or ear<br>Distinguish him from others he did keep<br>The deck, with glove or hat or handkerchief<br>Still waving, as the fits and stirs of ’s mind<br>Could best express how slow his [[Soul\|soul]] sailed on,<br>How swift his [[Ship\|ship]]. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Thou shouldst have made him<br>As little as a crow, or less, ere left<br>To after-eye him. |
| PISANIO | Madam, so I did. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I would have broke mine eye-strings, cracked them,<br>but<br>To look upon him till the diminution<br>Of space had pointed him sharp as my needle;<br>Nay, followed him till he had melted from<br>The smallness of a gnat to air, and then<br>Have turned mine eye and wept. But, good Pisanio,<br>When shall we hear from him? |
| PISANIO | Be assured, madam,<br>With his next vantage. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I did not take my leave of him, but had<br>Most pretty things to say. Ere I could tell him<br>How I would think on him at certain hours,<br>Such thoughts and such, or I could make him swear<br>The shes of [[Italy]] should not betray<br>Mine interest and his [[Honour\|honour]], or have charged him<br>At the sixth hour of morn, at [[Noon\|noon]], at midnight<br>T’encounter me with orisons—for then<br>I am in heaven for him—or ere I could<br>Give him that parting kiss which I had set<br>Betwixt two charming words, comes in my father,<br>And, like the tyrannous breathing of the north,<br>Shakes all our buds from growing. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | a Lady |
| LADY | The [[Queen]], madam,<br>Desires your highness’ company. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | (to Pisanio)<br>Those things I bid you do, get them dispatched.<br>I will attend the [[Queen]]. |
| PISANIO | Madam, I shall. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Innogen]] and Lady at one door, Pisanio at another |
| *(didascalia)* | 1.4 A table brought out, with a banquet upon it. Enter Filario, Giacomo, a Frenchman, a Dutchman, and a Spaniard |
| GIACOMO | Believe it, sir, I have seen him in Britain. He was then of a crescent note, expected to prove so worthy as since he hath been allowed the name of. But I could then have looked on him without the help of admiration, though the catalogue of his endowments had been tabled by his side and I to peruse him by items. |
| FILARIO | You speak of him when he was less furnished than now he is with that which makes him both without and within. |
| FRENCHMAN | I have seen him in France. We had very many there could behold the sun with as firm eyes as he. |
| GIACOMO | This matter of marrying his [[King\|king]]’s daughter, wherein he must be weighed rather by her value than his own, words him, I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not, a great deal from the matter. |
| FRENCHMAN | And then his banishment. |
| GIACOMO | Ay, and the approbation of those that weep this lamentable [[divorce]] under her colours are wonderfully to extend him, be it but to fortify her judgement, which else an easy battery might lay flat for taking a beggar without less quality. But how comes it he is to sojourn with you? How creeps acquaintance? |
| FILARIO | His father and I were soldiers together, to whom I have been often bound for no less than my life. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Posthumus]] |
| FILARIO | Here comes the Briton. Let him be so entertained amongst you as suits with gentlemen of your knowing to a stranger of his quality. I beseech you all, be better known to this gentleman, whom I commend to you as a noble friend of mine. How worthy he is I will leave to appear hereafter rather than story him in his own hearing. |
| FRENCHMAN | (to [[Posthumus]]) Sir, we have known together in Orléans. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Since when I have been debtor to you for courtesies which I will be ever to pay, and yet pay still. |
| FRENCHMAN | Sir, you o’er-rate my poor kindness. I was glad I did atone my countryman and you. It had been pity you should have been put together with so mortal a purpose as then each bore, upon importance of so slight and trivial a [[Nature\|nature]]. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | By your pardon, sir, I was then a young traveller, rather shunned to go even with what I heard than in my every action to be guided by others’ experiences; but upon my mended judgement—if I offend not to say it is mended—my quarrel was not altogether slight. |
| FRENCHMAN | [[Faith]], yes, to be put to the arbitrement of swords, and by such two that would by all likelihood have confounded one the other, or have fallen both. |
| GIACOMO | Can we with manners ask what was the difference? |
| FRENCHMAN | Safely, I think. ’Twas a contention in public, which may without contradiction suffer the report. It was much like an argument that fell out last [[Night\|night]], where each of us fell in praise of our country mistresses, this gentleman at that [[Time\|time]] vouching—and upon warrant of bloody affirmation—his to be more fair, virtuous, wise, chaste, constant, qualified, and less attemptable than any the rarest of our ladies in France. |
| GIACOMO | That lady is not now living, or this gentleman’s opinion by this worn out. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | She holds her virtue still, and I my mind. |
| GIACOMO | You must not so far prefer her fore ours of [[Italy]]. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Being so far provoked as I was in France I would abate her nothing, though I profess myself her adorer, not her friend. |
| GIACOMO | As fair and as good—a kind of hand-in-hand comparison—had been something too fair and too good for any lady in Britain. If she went before others I have seen—as that diamond of yours outlustres many I have beheld—I could not but believe she excelled many; but I have not seen the most precious diamond that is, nor you the lady. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | I praised her as I rated her; so do I my stone. |
| GIACOMO | What do you esteem it at? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | More than the world enjoys. |
| GIACOMO | Either your unparagoned mistress is dead, or she’s outprized by a trifle. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | You are mistaken. The one may be sold or given, or if there were wealth enough for the purchase or merit for the gift. The other is not a thing for sale, and only the gift of the gods. |
| GIACOMO | Which the gods have given you? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Which, by their graces, I will keep. |
| GIACOMO | You may wear her in title yours; but, you know, strange fowl light upon neighbouring ponds. Your ring may be stolen too; so your brace of unprizable estimations, the one is but frail, and the other casual. A cunning thief or a that-way accomplished courtier would hazard the winning both of first and last. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Your [[Italy]] contains none so accomplished a courtier to convince the [[Honour\|honour]] of my mistress if in the holding or loss of that you term her frail. I do nothing [[Doubt\|doubt]] you have store of thieves; notwithstanding, I [[Fear\|fear]] not my ring. |
| FILARIO | Let us leave here, gentlemen. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Sir, with all my heart. This worthy signor, I thank him, makes no stranger of me. We are familiar at first. |
| GIACOMO | With five times so much conversation I should get ground of your fair mistress, make her go back even to the yielding, had I admittance and opportunity to friend. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | No, no. |
| GIACOMO | I dare thereupon pawn the moiety of my estate to your ring, which in my opinion o’ervalues it something. But I make my wager rather against your confidence than her reputation, and, to bar your offence herein too, I durst attempt it against any lady in the world. in |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | You are a great deal abused in too bold a [[Persuasion\|persuasion]], and I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not you sustain what you’re worthy of by your attempt. |
| GIACOMO | What’s that? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | A repulse; though your attempt, as you call it, deserve more—a punishment, too. |
| FILARIO | Gentlemen, enough of this. It came in too suddenly. Let it die as it was born; and, I pray you, be better acquainted. |
| GIACOMO | Would I had put my estate and my neighbour’s on th’approbation of what I have spoke. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | What lady would you choose to assail? |
| GIACOMO | Yours, whom in constancy you think stands so safe. I will lay you ten thousand ducats to your ring that, commend me to the [[Court\|court]] where your lady is, with no more advantage than the opportunity of a second conference, and I will bring from thence that [[Honour\|honour]] of hers which you imagine so reserved. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | I will wage against your gold, gold to it; my ring I hold dear as my finger, ’tis part of it. |
| GIACOMO | You are a friend, and therein the wiser. If you buy ladies’ flesh at a million a dram, you cannot preserve it from tainting. But I see you have some religion in you, that you [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | This is but a custom in your tongue. You bear a graver purpose, I [[Hope\|hope]]. |
| GIACOMO | I am the master of my speeches, and would undergo what’s spoken, I swear. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Will you? I shall but lend my diamond till your return. Let there be covenants drawn between ’s. My mistress exceeds in goodness the hugeness of your unworthy thinking. I dare you to this match. Here’s my ring. |
| FILARIO | I will have it no lay. |
| GIACOMO | By the gods, it is one. If I bring you no sufficient testimony that I have enjoyed the dearest bodily part of your mistress, my ten thousand ducats are yours; so is your diamond too. If I come off and leave her in such [[Honour\|honour]] as you have trust in, she your [[Jewel\|jewel]], this your [[Jewel\|jewel]], and my gold are yours, provided I have your commendation for my more free entertainment. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | I embrace these conditions; let us have articles betwixt us. Only thus far you shall answer: if you make your voyage upon her and give me directly to understand you have prevailed, I am no further your enemy; she is not worth our [[debate]]. If she remain unseduced, you not making it appear otherwise, for your ill opinion and th’assault you have made to her chastity you shall answer me with your [[Sword\|sword]]. |
| GIACOMO | Your hand, a covenant. We will things set down by lawful counsel, and straight away for Britain, lest the bargain should catch cold and starve. I will fetch my gold and have our two wagers recorded. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Agreed. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with Giacomo |
| FRENCHMAN | Will this hold, think you? |
| FILARIO | Signor Giacomo will not from it. Pray let us follow ’em. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt. Table is removed |

### Atto 1, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Queen]], Ladies, and Cornelius, a doctor |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | Whiles yet the dew’s on ground, gather those [[Flowers\|flowers]].<br>Make haste. Who has the note of them?<br>A LADY<br>I, madam. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | Dispatch. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Ladies |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | Now, Master Doctor, have you brought those drugs? |
| CORNELIUS | Pleaseth your highness, ay. Here they are, madam.<br>He gives her a box<br>But I beseech your grace, without offence—<br>My [[Conscience\|conscience]] bids me ask—wherefore you have<br>Commanded of me these most poisonous compounds,<br>Which are the movers of a languishing [[Death\|death]],<br>But though slow, deadly. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | I [[Wonder\|wonder]], doctor,<br>Thou ask‘st me such a question. Have I not been<br>Thy pupil long? Hast thou not learned me how<br>To make perfumes, distil, preserve—yea, so<br>That our great [[King]] himself doth woo me oft<br>For my confections? Having thus far proceeded,<br>Unless thou think’st me devilish, is’t not meet<br>That I did amplify my judgement in<br>Other conclusions? I will try the forces<br>Of these thy compounds on such creatures as<br>We count not worth the hanging, but none human,<br>To try the vigour of them, and apply<br>Allayments to their act, and by them gather<br>Their several virtues and effects. |
| CORNELIUS | Your highness<br>Shall from this practice but make hard your heart.<br>Besides, the seeing these effects will be<br>Both noisome and infectious. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | O, content thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pisanio |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | (Aside) Here comes a flattering rascal; upon him<br>Will I first work. He’s factor for his master,<br>And enemy to my son. (Aloud) How now, Pisanio?—<br>Doctor, your service for this [[Time\|time]] is ended.<br>Take your own way. |
| CORNELIUS | (aside)<br>I do suspect you, madam.<br>But you shall do no harm. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | (to Pisanio)<br>Hark thee, a word. |
| CORNELIUS | (aside)<br>I do not like her. She doth think she has<br>Strange ling’ring poisons. I do know her spirit,<br>And will not trust one of her malice with<br>A drug of such damned [[Nature\|nature]]. Those she has<br>Will stupefy and dull the sense a while,<br>Which first, perchance, she’ll prove on cats and [[dogs]],<br>Then afterward up higher; but there is<br>No danger in what show of [[Death\|death]] it makes<br>More than the locking up the spirits a [[Time\|time]],<br>To be more fresh, reviving. She is fooled<br>With a most false effect, and I the truer<br>So to be false with her. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | No further service, doctor,<br>Until I send for thee. |
| CORNELIUS | I humbly take my leave. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | (to Pisanio)<br>Weeps she still, sayst thou? Dost thou think in [[Time\|time]]<br>She will not quench, and let instructions enter<br>Where folly now possesses? Do thou work.<br>When thou shalt bring me word she loves my son<br>I’ll tell thee on the instant thou art then<br>As great as is thy master—greater, for<br>His fortunes all lie speechless, and his name<br>Is at last gasp. Return he cannot, nor<br>Continue where he is. To shift his being<br>Is to exchange one misery with another,<br>And every day that comes comes to [[Decay\|decay]]<br>A day’s work in him. What shalt thou expect<br>To be depender on a thing that leans,<br>Who cannot be new built nor has no friends<br>So much as but to prop him? |
| *(didascalia)* | She drops her box. He takes it up |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | Thou tak’st up<br>Thou know‘st not what; but take it for thy labour.<br>It is a thing I made which hath the [[King]]<br>Five times redeemed from [[Death\|death]]. I do not know<br>What is more cordial. Nay, I prithee take it.<br>It is an earnest of a farther good<br>That I mean to thee. Tell thy mistress how<br>The case stands with her; do’t as from thyself.<br>Think what a chance thou changest on, but think<br>Thou hast thy mistress still; to boot, my son,<br>Who shall take notice of thee. I’ll move the [[King]]<br>To any shape of thy preferment, such<br>As thou’lt [[Desire\|desire]]; and then myself, I chiefly,<br>That set thee on to this [[Desert\|desert]], am bound<br>To load thy merit richly. Call my women.<br>Think on my words. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Pisanio |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | A sly and constant knave,<br>Not to be shaked; the agent for his master,<br>And the remembrancer of her to hold<br>The hand-fast to her lord. I have given him that<br>Which, if he take, shall quite unpeople her<br>Of liegers for her sweet, and which she after,<br>Except she bend her [[Humour\|humour]], shall be assured<br>To taste of too. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pisanio and Ladies |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | So, so; [[Well\|well]] done, [[Well\|well]] done.<br>The violets, cowslips, and the primroses<br>Bear to my closet. Fare thee [[Well\|well]], Pisanio.<br>Think on my words, Pisanio. |
| PISANIO | And shall do. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Queen]] and Ladies |
| PISANIO | But when to my good lord I prove untrue,<br>I’ll choke mysetf—there’s all I’ll do for you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 1, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Innogen]] |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | A father cruel and a stepdame false,<br>A foolish suitor to a wedded lady<br>That hath her husband banished. O, that husband,<br>My supreme [[Crown\|crown]] of grief, and those repeated<br>Vexations of it! Had I been thief-stol‘n,<br>As my two brothers, happy; but most miserable<br>Is the [[Desire\|desire]] that’s glorious. Blest be those,<br>How mean soe’er, that have their honest wills,<br>Which [[Seasons\|seasons]] comfort. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pisanio and Giacomo |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Who may this be? Fie! |
| PISANIO | Madam, a noble gentleman of Rome<br>Comes from my lord with letters. |
| GIACOMO | Change you, madam?<br>The worthy Leonatus is in safety,<br>And greets your highness dearly.<br>He gives her the letters |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Thanks, good sir.<br>You’re kindly welcome.<br>She reads the letters |
| GIACOMO | (aside)<br>All of her that is out of door most rich!<br>If she be furnished with a mind so rare<br>She is [[Alone\|alone]], th’Arabian bird, and I<br>Have lost the wager. Boldness be my friend;<br>Arm me audacity from head to foot,<br>Or, like the Parthian, I shall flying fight;<br>Rather, directly fly. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | (reads aloud) ’He is one of the noblest note, to whose kindnesses I am most infinitely tied. Reflect upon him accordingly, as you value<br>Your truest<br>Leonatus.’<br>(To Giacomo) So far I read aloud,<br>But even the very middle of my heart<br>Is warmed by th’ rest, and takes it thankfully.<br>You are as welcome, worthy sir, as I<br>Have words to bid you, and shall find it so<br>In all that I can do. |
| GIACOMO | Thanks, fairest lady.<br>What, are men mad? Hath [[Nature\|nature]] given them eyes<br>To see this vaulted arch and the rich crop<br>Of sea and land, which can distinguish ‘twixt<br>The fiery orbs above and the twinned stones<br>Upon th’unnumbered beach, and can we not<br>Partition make with spectacles so precious<br>’Twixt fair and foul? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What makes your admiration? |
| GIACOMO | It cannot be i‘th’ eye—for apes and monkeys,<br>’Twixt two such shes, would chatter this way and<br>Contemn with mows the other; nor i‘th’ judgement,<br>For idiots in this case of favour would<br>Be wisely definite; nor i’th’ appetite—<br>Sluttery, to such neat excellence opposed,<br>Should make [[Desire\|desire]] vomit emptiness,<br>Not so allured to feed. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What is the matter, trow? |
| GIACOMO | The cloyed will,<br>That satiate yet unsatisfied [[Desire\|desire]], that tub<br>Both filled and running, ravening first the [[Lamb\|lamb]],<br>Longs after for the garbage. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What, dear sir,<br>Thus raps you? Are you [[Well\|well]]? |
| GIACOMO | Thanks, madam, [[Well\|well]]. (To Pisanio) Beseech you, sir,<br>[[Desire]] my man’s abode where I did leave him.<br>He’s strange and peevish. |
| PISANIO | I was going, sir,<br>To give him welcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Continues [[Well\|well]] my lord?<br>His health, beseech you? |
| GIACOMO | [[Well]], madam. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Is he disposed to mirth? I [[Hope\|hope]] he is. |
| GIACOMO | Exceeding pleasant, none a stranger there<br>So merry and so gamesome. He is called<br>The Briton Reveller. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | When he was here<br>He did incline to sadness, and oft-times<br>Not knowing why. |
| GIACOMO | I never saw him sad.<br>There is a Frenchman his companion, one<br>An eminent monsieur that, it seems, much loves<br>A Gallian girl at [[Home\|home]]. He furnaces<br>The thick sighs from him, whiles the jolly Briton—<br>Your lord, I mean—laughs from ’s free lungs,<br>cries ‘O,<br>Can my sides hold, to think that man, who knows<br>By [[history]], report or his own proof<br>What woman is, yea, what she cannot choose<br>But must be, will ’s free hours languish<br>For assured bondage?’ |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Will my lord say so? |
| GIACOMO | Ay, madam, with his eyes in flood with laughter.<br>It is a recreation to be by<br>And hear him mock the Frenchman. But heavens<br>know<br>Some men are much to blame. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Not he, I [[Hope\|hope]]. |
| GIACOMO | Not he; but yet heaven’s bounty towards him might<br>Be used more thankfully. In himself ’tis much;<br>In you, which I count his, beyond all talents.<br>Whilst I am bound to [[Wonder\|wonder]], I am bound<br>To pity too. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What do you pity, sir? |
| GIACOMO | Two creatures heartily. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Am I one, sir?<br>You look on me; what wreck discern you in me<br>Deserves your pity? |
| GIACOMO | Lamentablel What,<br>To hide me from the radiant sun, and solace<br>I’th’ dungeon by a snuff? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I pray you, sir,<br>Deliver with more openness your answers<br>To my demands. Why do you pity me? |
| GIACOMO | That others do—<br>I was about to say enjoy your—but<br>It is an office of the gods to venge it,<br>Not mine to speak on’t. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | You do seem to know<br>Something of me, or what concerns me. Pray you,<br>Since doubting things go ill often hurts more<br>Than to be sure they do—for certainties<br>Either are past remedies, or, timely knowing,<br>The remedy then born—discover to me<br>What both you spur and stop. |
| GIACOMO | Had I this cheek<br>To bathe my lips upon; this hand whose touch,<br>Whose every touch, would force the feeler’s [[Soul\|soul]]<br>To th’oath of loyalty; this object which<br>Takes prisoner the wild motion of mine eye,<br>Firing it only here: should I, damned then,<br>Slaver with lips as common as the stairs<br>That mount the Capitol; join grips with hands<br>Made hard with hourly falsehood—faisehood as<br>With labour; then by-peeping in an eye<br>Base and illustrous as the smoky light<br>That’s fed with stinking tallow—it were fit<br>That all the plagues of hell should at one [[Time\|time]]<br>Encounter such revolt. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | My lord, I [[Fear\|fear]],<br>Has forgot Britain. |
| GIACOMO | And himself. Not I<br>Inclined to this intelligence pronounce<br>The beggary of his change, but ’tis your graces<br>That from my mutest [[Conscience\|conscience]] to my tongue<br>Charms this report out. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Let me hear no more. |
| GIACOMO | O dearest [[Soul\|soul]], your cause doth strike my heart<br>With pity that doth make me sick. A lady<br>So fair, and fastened to an empery<br>Would make the great’st [[King\|king]] [[Double\|double]], to be partnered<br>With tomboys hired with that [[Self\|self]] exhibition<br>Which your own coffers yield; with diseased ventures<br>That play with all infirmities for gold<br>Which rottenness can lend to [[Nature\|nature]]; such boiled stuff<br>As [[Well\|well]] might [[Poison\|poison]] [[Poison\|poison]]! Be revenged,<br>Or she that bore you was no [[Queen\|queen]], and you<br>Recoil from your great stock. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Revenged?<br>How should I be revenged? If this be true—<br>As I have such a heart that both mine ears<br>Must not in haste abuse—if it be true,<br>How should I be revenged? |
| GIACOMO | Should he make me<br>Live like [[Diana]]’s priest betwixt cold sheets<br>Whiles he is vaulting variable ramps,<br>In your despite, upon your purse—[[Revenge\|revenge]] it.<br>I dedicate myself to your sweet pleasure,<br>More noble than that runagate to your bed,<br>And will continue fast to your affection,<br>Still close as sure. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What ho, Pisanio! |
| GIACOMO | Let me my service tender on your lips. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Away, I do condemn mine ears that have<br>So long attended thee. If thou wert honourable<br>Thou wouldst have told this tale for virtue, not<br>For such an end thou seek‘st, as base as strange.<br>Thou wrong’st a gentleman who is as far<br>From thy report as thou from [[Honour\|honour]], and<br>Solicit’st here a lady that disdains<br>Thee and the devil alike. What ho, Pisanio!<br>The [[King]] my father shall be made acquainted<br>Of thy assault. If he shall think it fit<br>A saucy stranger in his [[Court\|court]] to mart<br>As in a Romish stew, and to expound<br>His beastly mind to us, he hath a [[Court\|court]]<br>He little cares for, and a daughter who<br>He not respects at all. What ho, Pisanio! |
| GIACOMO | O happy Leonatus! I may say<br>The credit that thy lady hath of thee<br>Deserves thy trust, and thy most perfect goodness<br>Her assured credit. Blessed live you long,<br>A lady to the worthiest sir that ever<br>Country called his; and you his mistress, only<br>For the most worthiest fit. Give me your pardon.<br>I have spoke this to know if your affiance<br>Were deeply rooted, and shall make your lord<br>That which he is new o’er; and he is one<br>The truest mannered, such a holy [[Witch\|witch]]<br>That he enchants societies into him;<br>Half all men’s hearts are his. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | You make amends. |
| GIACOMO | He sits ’mongst men like a descended god.<br>He hath a kind of [[Honour\|honour]] sets him off<br>More than a mortal seeming. Be not angry,<br>Most mighty princess, that I have adventured<br>To try your taking of a false report, which hath<br>Honoured with confirmation your great judgement<br>In the election of a sir so rare<br>Which you know cannot err. The [[Love\|love]] I bear him<br>Made me to fan you thus, but the gods made you,<br>Unlike all others, chaffless. Pray, your pardon. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | All’s [[Well\|well]], sir. Take my power i’th’ [[Court\|court]] for yours. |
| GIACOMO | My humble thanks. I had almost forgot<br>T’entreat your grace but in a small request,<br>And yet of moment too, for it concerns<br>Your lord; myself and other noble friends<br>Are partners in the business. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Pray what is’t? |
| GIACOMO | Some dozen Romans of us, and your lord—<br>Best feather of our wing—have mingled sums<br>To buy a present for the Emperor,<br>Which I, the factor for the rest, have done<br>In France. ’Tis plate of rare device, and jewels<br>Of rich and exquisite form; their value’s great,<br>And I am something curious, being strange,<br>To have them in safe stowage. May it please you<br>To take them in protection? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Willingly,<br>And pawn mine [[Honour\|honour]] for their safety; since<br>My lord hath interest in them, I will keep them<br>In my bedchamber. |
| GIACOMO | They are in a trunk<br>Attended by my men. I will make bold<br>To send them to you, only for this [[Night\|night]].<br>I must aboard tomorrow. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | O, no, no! |
| GIACOMO | Yes, I beseech, or I shall short my word<br>By length’ning my return. From Gallia<br>I crossed the seas on purpose and on promise<br>To see your grace. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I thank you for your pains;<br>But not away tomorrow! |
| GIACOMO | O, I must, madam.<br>Therefore I shall beseech you, if you please<br>To greet your lord with writing, do’t tonight.<br>I have outstood my [[Time\|time]], which is material<br>To th’ tender of our present. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I will write.<br>Send your trunk to me, it shall safe be kept,<br>And truly yielded you. You’re very welcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 2, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Cloten]] and the two Lords |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Was there ever man had such luck? When I kissed the jack upon an upcast, to be hit away! I had a hundred pound on’t, and then a whoreson jackanapes must take me up for swearing, as if I borrowed mine oaths of him, and might not spend them at my pleasure. |
| FIRST LORD | What got he by that? You have broke his pate with your bowl. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) If his wit had been like him that broke it, it would have run all out. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | When a gentleman is disposed to swear it is not for any standers-by to curtail his oaths, ha? |
| SECOND LORD | No, my lord (aside)—nor crop the ears of them. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Whoreson dog! I give him satisfaction? Would he had been one of my rank. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) To have smelled like a [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I am not vexed more at anything in th‘earth. A pox on’t, I had rather not be so noble as I am. They dare not fight with me because of the [[Queen]], my mother. Every jack-slave hath his bellyful of fighting, and I must go up and down like a cock that nobody can match. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) You are cock and capon too an you crow cock with your comb on. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Sayst thou? |
| SECOND LORD | It is not fit your lordship should undertake every companion that you give offence to. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | No, I know that, but it is fit I should commit offence to my inferiors. |
| SECOND LORD | Ay, it is fit for your lordship only. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Why, so I say. |
| FIRST LORD | Did you hear of a stranger that’s come to [[Court\|court]] tonight? |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | A stranger, and I not know on’t? |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) He’s a strange fellow himself and knows it not. |
| FIRST LORD | There’s an Italian come, and, ’tis thought, one of Leonatus’ friends. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Leonatus? A banished rascal; and he’s another, whatsoever he be. Who told you of this stranger? |
| FIRST LORD | One of your lordship’s pages. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Is it fit I went to look upon him? Is there no derogation in’t? |
| SECOND LORD | You cannot derogate, my lord. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Not easily, I think. |
| SECOND LORD | (aside) You are a [[Fool\|fool]] granted, therefore your issues, being foolish, do not derogate. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Come, I’ll go see this Italian. What I have lost today at bowls I’ll win tonight of him. Come, go. |
| SECOND LORD | I’ll attend your lordship. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Cloten]] and First Lord |
| SECOND LORD | That such a crafty devil as is his mother<br>Should yield the world this ass!—a woman that<br>Bears all down with her brain, and this her son<br>Cannot take two from twenty, for his heart,<br>And leave eighteen. Alas, poor princess,<br>Thou divine [[Innogen]], what thou endur‘st,<br>Betwixt a father by thy stepdame governed,<br>A mother hourly coining plots, a wooer<br>More hateful than the foul expulsion is<br>Of thy dear husband, than that horrid act<br>Of the [[divorce]] he’d make! The heavens hold firm<br>The walls of thy dear [[Honour\|honour]], keep unshaked<br>That temple, thy fair mind, that thou mayst stand<br>T’enjoy thy banished lord and this great land! Exit |

### Atto 2, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | A trunk and arras. A bed is thrust forth with [[Innogen]] in it, reading a book. Enter to her [[Helen]], a lady |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Who’s there? My woman [[Helen]]? |
| [[Helen\|HELEN]] | Please you, madam. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | What hour is it? |
| [[Helen\|HELEN]] | Almost midnight, madam. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I have read three hours then. Mine eyes are weak.<br>Fold down the leaf where I have left. To bed.<br>Take not away the taper; leave it burning,<br>And if thou canst awake by four o’th’ [[Clock\|clock]],<br>I prithee call me. [[Sleep]] hath seized me wholly. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Helen]] |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | To your protection I commend me, gods.<br>From fairies and the tempters of the [[Night\|night]]<br>Guard me, beseech ye.<br>She sleeps.<br>Giacomo comes from the trunk |
| GIACOMO | The crickets sing, and man’s o‘er-laboured sense<br>Repairs itself by rest. Our [[Tarquin]] thus<br>Did softly press the rushes ere he wakened<br>The chastity he wounded. Cytherea,<br>How bravely thou becom’st thy bed! Fresh lily,<br>And whiter than the sheets! That I might touch,<br>But kiss, one kiss! Rubies unparagoned,<br>How dearly they do‘t! ’Tis her breathing that<br>Perfumes the chamber thus. The flame o‘th’ taper<br>Bows toward her, and would underpeep her lids,<br>To see th’enclosed lights, now canopied<br>Under these windows, white and azure-laced<br>With blue of heaven’s own tinct. But my design-<br>To note the chamber. I will write all down.<br>He writes in his tables<br>Such and such pictures, there the [[Window\|window]], such<br>Th‘adornment of her bed, the arras, figures,<br>Why, such and such; and the contents o’th’ story.<br>Ah, but some natural notes about her [[Body\|body]]<br>Above ten thousand meaner movables<br>Would testify t’enrich mine inventory.<br>O [[Sleep\|sleep]], thou ape of [[Death\|death]], lie dull upon her,<br>And be her sense but as a monument<br>Thus in a chapel lying. Come off, come off;<br>As slippery as the Gordian knot was hard.<br>He takes the bracelet from her arm<br>‘Tis mine, and this will witness outwardly,<br>As strongly as the [[Conscience\|conscience]] does within,<br>To th’ madding of her lord. On her left breast<br>A mole, cinque-spotted, like the crimson drops<br>I’th’ [[Bottom\|bottom]] of a cowslip. Here’s a voucher<br>Stronger than ever law could make. This secret<br>Will force him think I have picked the lock and<br>ta’en<br>The treasure of her [[Honour\|honour]]. No more. To what end?<br>Why should I write this down that’s riveted,<br>Screwed to my [[Memory\|memory]]? She hath been reading late,<br>The tale of Tereus. Here the leaf’s turned down<br>Where Philomel gave up. I have enough.<br>To th’ trunk again, and shut the [[spring]] of it.<br>Swift, swift, you dragons of the [[Night\|night]], that dawning<br>May bare [[The Raven\|the raven]]’s eye! I lodge in [[Fear\|fear]].<br>Though this’ a heavenly angel, hell is here.<br>[[Clock]] strikes<br>One, two, three. [[Time]], [[Time\|time]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit into the trunk. The bed and trunk are removed |

### Atto 2, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Cloten]] and the two Lords |
| FIRST LORD | Your lordship is the most patient man in loss, the most coldest that ever turned up ace. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | It would make any man cold to lose. |
| FIRST LORD | But not every man patient after the noble temper of your lordship. You are most hot and furious when you win. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Winning will put any man into [[Courage\|courage]]. If I could get this foolish [[Innogen]] I should have gold enough. It’s almost morning, is’t not? |
| FIRST LORD | Day, my lord. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I would this music would come. I am advised to give her music o’ mornings; they say it will penetrate. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Musicians |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Come on, tune. If you can penetrate her with your fingering, so; we’ll try with tongue too. If none will do, let her remain; but I’ll never give o’er. First, a very excellent good-conceited thing; after, a wonderful sweet air with admirable rich words to it; and then let her consider. |
| *(didascalia)* | Music |
| *(didascalia)* | MUSICIAN (sings) |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Hark, hark, the lark at heaven gate sings,<br>And Phoebus gins arise,<br>His steeds to [[Water\|water]] at those springs<br>On chaticed [[Flowers\|flowers]] that lies,<br>And winking Mary-buds begin to ope their golden eyes;<br>With everything that pretty is, my lady sweet, arise,<br>Arise, arise! |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | So, get you gone. If this penetrate I will consider your music the better; if it do not, it is a vice in her ears which [[Horse\|horse]] hairs and calves’ guts nor the voice of unpaved eunuch to boot can never amend. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Musicians |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Cymbeline]] and the [[Queen]] |
| SECOND LORD | Here comes the [[King]]. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I am glad I was up so late, for that’s the reason I was up so early. He cannot choose but take this service I have done fatherly. Good morrow to your majesty, and to my gracious mother. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | Attend you here the door of our stern daughter?<br>Will she not forth? |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I have assailed her with musics, but she vouchsafes no notice. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | The [[Exile\|exile]] of her minion is too new.<br>She hath not yet forgot him. Some more [[Time\|time]]<br>Must wear the print of his remembrance out,<br>And then she’s yours. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | (to [[Cloten]]) You are most bound to th’ [[King]],<br>Who lets go by no vantages that may<br>Prefer you to his daughter. Frame yourself<br>To orderly solicits, and be friended<br>With aptness of the season. Make denials<br>Increase your services; so seem as if<br>You were inspired to do those duties which<br>You tender to her; that you in all obey her,<br>Save when command to your dismission tends,<br>And therein you are senseless. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Senseless? Not so. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | (to [[Cymbeline]])<br>So like you, sir, ambassadors from Rome;<br>The one is Caius [[Lucius]]. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | A worthy fellow,<br>Albeit he comes on angry purpose now:<br>But that’s no fault of his. We must receive him<br>According to the [[Honour\|honour]] of his sender,<br>And towards himself, his goodness forespent on us,<br>We must extend our notice. Our dear son,<br>When you have given good morning to your mistress,<br>Attend the [[Queen]] and us. We shall have need<br>T’employ you towards this Roman. Come, our [[Queen\|queen]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | [[Cloten]] |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | If she be up, I’ll speak with her; if not,<br>Let her lie still and dream. |
| *(didascalia)* | He knocks |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | By your leave, ho!—<br>I know her women are about her; what<br>If I do line one of their hands? ‘Tis gold<br>Which buys admittance—oft it doth—yea, and makes<br>[[Diana]]’s rangers false themselves, yield up<br>Their deer to th’ stand o’th’ stealer; and ’tis gold<br>Which makes the true man killed and saves the thief,<br>Nay, sometime hangs both thief and true man. What<br>Can it not do and undo? I will make<br>One of her women lawyer to me, for<br>I yet not understand the case myself.—<br>By your leave.<br>Knocks. Enter a Lady |
| LADY | Who’s there that knocks? |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | A gentleman. |
| LADY | No more? |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Yes, and a gentlewoman’s son. |
| LADY | That’s more |
| *(didascalia)* | Aside Than some whose tailors are as dear as |
| LADY | yours<br>Can justly boast of. (To him) What’s your lordship’s<br>pleasure? |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Your lady’s person. Is she ready? |
| LADY | Ay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Aside To keep her chamber. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | There is gold for you.<br>Sell me your good report. |
| LADY | How, my good name?—or to report of you<br>What I shall think is good? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| LADY | [[Innogen]]<br>The Princess. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Good morrow, fairest. Sister, your sweet hand. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Good morrow, sir. You lay out too much pains<br>For purchasing but trouble. The thanks I give<br>Is telling you that I am poor of thanks,<br>And scarce can spare them. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Still I swear I [[Love\|love]] you. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | If you but said so, ’twere as deep with me.<br>If you swear still, your recompense is still<br>That I regard it not. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | This is no answer. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | But that you shall not say I yield being silent,<br>I would not speak. I pray you, spare me. [[Faith]],<br>I shall unfold equal discourtesy<br>To your best kindness. One of your great knowing<br>Should learn, being taught, forbearance. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | To leave you in your [[Madness\|madness]], ’twere my sin.<br>I will not. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Fools cure not mad folks. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Do you call me [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | As I am mad, I do.<br>If you’ll be patient, I’ll no more be mad;<br>That cures us both. I am much sorry, sir,<br>You put me to forget a lady’s manners<br>By being so verbal; and learn now for all<br>That I, which know my heart, do here pronounce<br>By th’ very [[Truth\|truth]] of it: I care not for you,<br>And am so near the lack of [[Charity\|charity]]<br>To accuse myself I hate you, which I had rather<br>You felt than make’t my boast. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | You sin against<br>Obedience which you owe your father. For<br>The contract you pretend with that base wretch,<br>One bred of alms and fostered with cold dishes,<br>With scraps o‘th’ [[Court\|court]], it is no contract, none.<br>And though it be allowed in meaner parties—<br>Yet who than he more mean?—to knit their souls,<br>On whom there is no more dependency<br>But brats and beggary, in [[Self\|self]]-figured knot,<br>Yet you are curbed from that enlargement by<br>The consequence o’th’ [[Crown\|crown]], and must not foil<br>The precious note of it with a base slave,<br>A hilding for a livery, a squire’s cloth,<br>A pantler—not so eminent. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Profane fellow,<br>Wert thou the son of Jupiter, and no more<br>But what thou art besides, thou wert too base<br>To be his groom; thou wert dignified enough,<br>Even to the point of envy, if ’twere made<br>Comparative for your virtues to be styled<br>The under-hangman of his kingdom, and hated<br>For being preferred so [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | The south-fog rot him! |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | He never can meet more mischance than come<br>To be but named of thee. His meanest garment<br>That ever hath but clipped his [[Body\|body]] is dearer<br>In my respect than all the hairs above thee,<br>Were they all made such men. How now, Pisanio! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pisanio |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | His garment? Now the devil— |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | (to Pisanio)<br>To Dorothy, my woman, hie thee presently. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | His garment? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | (to Pisanio) I am sprited with a [[Fool\|fool]],<br>Frighted, and angered worse. Go bid my woman<br>Search for a [[Jewel\|jewel]] that too casually<br>Hath left mine arm. It was thy master’s. ‘Shrew me<br>If I would lose it for a revenue<br>Of any [[King\|king]]’s in Europe! I do think<br>I saw’t this morning; confident I am<br>Last [[Night\|night]] ’twas on mine arm; I kissed it.<br>I [[Hope\|hope]] it be not gone to tell my lord<br>That I kiss aught but he. |
| PISANIO | ’Twill not be lost. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | I [[Hope\|hope]] so. Go and search. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Pisanio |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | You have abused me.<br>‘His meanest garment’? |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Ay, I said so, sir.<br>If you will make’t an action, call witness to’t. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I will inform your father. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Your mother too.<br>She’s my good lady, and will conceive, I [[Hope\|hope]],<br>But the worst of me. So I leave you, sir,<br>To th’ worst of discontent. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | I’ll be revenged.<br>‘His meanest garment’? [[Well]]! Exit |

### Atto 2, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Posthumus]] and Filario |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | [[Fear]] it not, sir. I would I were so sure<br>To win the [[King]] as I am bold her [[Honour\|honour]]<br>Will remain hers. |
| FILARIO | What means do you make to him? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Not any; but abide the change of [[Time\|time]],<br>Quake in the present [[Winter\|winter]]’s state, and wish<br>That warmer days would come. In these seared hopes<br>I barely gratify your [[Love\|love]]; they failing,<br>I must die much your debtor. |
| FILARIO | Your very goodness and your company<br>O‘erpays all I can do. By this, your [[King\|king]]<br>Hath heard of great Augustus. Caius [[Lucius]]<br>Will do ’s commission throughly. And I think<br>He’ll grant the tribute, send th’arrearages,<br>Ere look upon our Romans, whose remembrance<br>Is yet fresh in their grief. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | I do believe,<br>Statist though I am none, nor like to be,<br>That this will prove a war, and you shall hear<br>The legions now in Gallia sooner landed<br>In our not-fearing Britain than have tidings<br>Of any penny tribute paid. Our countrymen<br>Are men more ordered than when [[Julius Caesar]]<br>Smiled at their lack of skill but found their [[Courage\|courage]]<br>Worthy his frowning at. Their discipline,<br>Now wing-led with their [[Courage\|courage]], will make known<br>To their approvers they are people such<br>That mend upon the world. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Giacomo |
| FILARIO | See, Giacomo. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | (to Giacomo)<br>The swiftest harts have posted you by land,<br>And winds of all the corners kissed your sails<br>To make your vessel nimble. |
| FILARIO | (to Giacomo)<br>Welcome, sir. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | (to Giacomo)<br>I [[Hope\|hope]] the briefness of your answer made<br>The speediness of your return. |
| GIACOMO | Your lady is<br>One of the fair’st that I have looked upon— |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | And therewithal the best, or let her [[Beauty\|beauty]]<br>Look through a casement to allure false hearts,<br>And be false with them. |
| GIACOMO | Here are letters for you. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Their tenor good, I trust. |
| GIACOMO | ’Tis very like.<br>[[Posthumus]] reads the letters |
| *(didascalia)* | FILARIO |
| GIACOMO | Was Caius [[Lucius]] in the Briton [[Court\|court]]<br>When you were there? |
| GIACOMO | He was expected then,<br>But not approached. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | All is [[Well\|well]] yet.<br>Sparkles this stone as it was wont, or is’t not<br>Too dull for your good wearing? |
| GIACOMO | If I had lost it<br>I should have lost the worth of it in gold.<br>I’ll make a [[Journey\|journey]] twice as far t’enjoy<br>A second [[Night\|night]] of such sweet shortness which<br>Was mine in Britain; for the ring is won. |
| POSTHUMOUS | The stone’s too hard to come by. |
| GIACOMO | Not a whit,<br>Your lady being so easy. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Make not, sir,<br>Your loss your sport. I [[Hope\|hope]] you know that we<br>Must not continue friends. |
| GIACOMO | Good sir, we must,<br>If you keep covenant. Had I not brought<br>The knowledge of your mistress [[Home\|home]] I grant<br>We were to question farther, but I now<br>Profess myself the winner of her [[Honour\|honour]],<br>Together with your ring, and not the wronger<br>Of her or you, having proceeded but<br>By both your wills. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | If you can make’t apparent<br>That you have tasted her in bed, my hand<br>And ring is yours. If not, the foul opinion<br>You had of her pure [[Honour\|honour]] gains or loses<br>Your [[Sword\|sword]] or mine, or masterless [[Leaves\|leaves]] both<br>To who shall find them. |
| GIACOMO | Sir, my circumstances,<br>Being so near the [[Truth\|truth]] as I will make them,<br>Must first induce you to believe; whose strength<br>I will confirm with oath, which I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not<br>You’ll give me leave to spare when you shall find<br>You need it not. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Proceed. |
| GIACOMO | First, her bedchamber—<br>Where I confess I slept not, but profess<br>Had that was [[Well\|well]] worth watching—it was hanged<br>With tapestry of silk and silver; the story<br>Proud [[Cleopatra]] when she met her Roman,<br>And Cydnus swelled above the banks, or for<br>The press of boats or [[Pride\|pride]]: a piece of work<br>So bravely done, so rich, that it did strive<br>In workmanship and value; which I wondered<br>Could be so rarely and exactly wrought,<br>Such the true life on’t was. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | This is true,<br>And this you might have heard of here, by me<br>Or by some other. |
| GIACOMO | More particulars<br>Must justify my knowledge. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | So they must,<br>Or do your [[Honour\|honour]] injury. |
| GIACOMO | The chimney<br>Is south the chamber, and the chimney-piece<br>Chaste Dian bathing. Never saw I figures<br>So likely to report themselves; the cutter<br>Was as another [[Nature\|nature]]; dumb, outwent her,<br>Motion and breath left out. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | This is a thing<br>Which you might from relation likewise reap,<br>Being, as it is, much spoke of. |
| GIACOMO | The roof o’th’ chamber<br>With golden cherubins is fretted. Her andirons—<br>I had forgot them—were two winking Cupids<br>Of silver, each on one foot standing, nicely<br>Depending on their brands. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | This is her [[Honour\|honour]]!<br>Let it be granted you have seen all this—and praise<br>Be given to your remembrance—the description<br>Of what is in her chamber nothing saves<br>The wager you have laid. |
| GIACOMO | Then, if you can<br>Be pale, I beg but leave to air this [[Jewel\|jewel]]. See!<br>He shows the bracelet<br>And now ’tis up again; it must be married<br>To that your diamond. I’ll keep them. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Jove!<br>Once more let me behold it. Is it that<br>Which I left with her? |
| GIACOMO | Sir, I thank her, that.<br>She stripped it from her arm. I see her yet.<br>Her pretty action did outsell her gift,<br>And yet enriched it too. She gave it me,<br>And said she prized it once. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Maybe she plucked it off<br>To send it me. |
| GIACOMO | She writes so to you, doth she? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | O, no, no, no—’tis true! Here, take this too.<br>He gives Giacomo his ring<br>It is a basilisk unto mine eye,<br>Kills me to look on’t. Let there be no [[Honour\|honour]]<br>Where there is [[Beauty\|beauty]], [[Truth\|truth]] where semblance, [[Love\|love]]<br>Where there’s another man. The vows of women<br>Of no more bondage be to where they are made<br>Than they are to their virtues, which is nothing!<br>O, above measure false! |
| PILASIO | Have patience, sir,<br>And take your ring again; ’tis not yet won.<br>It may be probable she lost it, or<br>Who knows if one her woman, being corrupted,<br>Hath stol’n it from her? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Very true,<br>And so I [[Hope\|hope]] he came by’t. Back my ring.<br>He takes his ring again<br>Render to me some corporal sign about her<br>More evident than this; for this was stol’n. |
| GIACOMO | By Jupiter, I had it from her arm. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Hark you, he swears, by Jupiter he swears.<br>‘Tis true, nay, keep the ring, ’tis true. I am sure<br>She would not lose it. Her attendants are<br>All sworn and honourable. They induced to steal it?<br>And by a stranger? No, he hath enjoyed her.<br>The cognizance of her incontinency<br>Is this. She hath bought the name of whore thus<br>dearly.<br>He gives Giacomo his ring<br>There, take thy hire, and all the fiends of hell<br>Divide themselves between you! |
| FILARIO | Sir, be patient.<br>This is not strong enough to be believed<br>Of one persuaded [[Well\|well]] of. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Never talk on’t.<br>She hath been colted by him. |
| GIACOMO | If you seek<br>For further satisfying, under her breast—<br>Worthy the pressing—lies a mole, right proud<br>Of that most delicate lodging. By my life,<br>I kissed it, and it gave me present hunger<br>To feed again, though full. You do remember<br>This stain upon her? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Ay, and it doth confirm<br>Another stain as big as hell can hold,<br>Were there no more but it. |
| GIACOMO | Will you hear more? |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Spare your arithmetic, never count the turns.<br>Once, and a million! |
| GIACOMO | I’ll be sworn. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | No swearing.<br>If you will swear you have not done‘t, you lie,<br>And I will kill thee if thou dost deny<br>Thou’st made me cuckold.<br>GlACOMO<br>I’ll deny nothing. |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | O that I had her here to tear her limb-meal!<br>I will go there and do’t i’th’ [[Court\|court]], before<br>Her father. I’ll do something. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| FILARIO | Quite besides<br>The [[government]] of patience! You have won.<br>Let’s follow and pervert the present wrath<br>He hath against himself. |
| GIACOMO | With all my heart. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 2, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Posthumus]] |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Is there no way for men to be, but women<br>Must be half-workers? We are bastards all,<br>And that most venerable man which I<br>Did call my father was I know not where<br>When I was stamped. Some coiner with his tools<br>Made me a counterfeit; yet my mother seemed<br>The Dian of that [[Time\|time]]: so doth my wife<br>The nonpareil of this. O vengeance, vengeance!<br>Me of my lawful pleasure she restrained,<br>And prayed me oft forbearance; did it with<br>A pudency so rosy the sweet view on’t<br>Might [[Well\|well]] have warmed old [[Saturn]]; that I thought<br>her<br>As chaste as unsunned [[Snow\|snow]]. O all the devils!<br>This yellow Giacomo in an hour—was’t not?—<br>Or less—at first? Perchance he spoke not, but<br>Like a full-acorned boar, a German one,<br>Cried ‘O!’ and mounted; found no opposition<br>But what he looked for should oppose and she<br>Should from encounter guard. Could I find out<br>The woman’s part in me—for there’s no motion<br>That tends to vice in man but I affirm<br>It is the woman’s part; be it lying, note it,<br>The woman’s; flattering, hers; deceiving, hers;<br>Lust and rank thoughts, hers, hers; revenges, hers;<br>Ambitions, covetings, change of prides, disdain,<br>Nice [[Longing\|longing]], slanders, [[Mutability\|mutability]],<br>All faults that man can name, nay, that hell knows,<br>Why, hers in part or all, but rather all—<br>For even to vice<br>They are not constant, but are changing still<br>One vice but of a minute old for one<br>Not half so old as that. I’ll write against them,<br>Detest them, curse them, yet ’tis greater skill<br>In a true hate to pray they have their will.<br>The very devils cannot plague them better. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | 3.1 Flourish. Enter in state [[Cymbeline]], the [[Queen]], [[Cloten]], and lords at one door, and at another, Caius [[Lucius]] and attendants |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | Now say, what would Augustus Caesar with us? |
| [[Lucius\|LUCIUS]] | When [[Julius Caesar]]—whose remembrance yet<br>Lives in men’s eyes, and will to ears and tongues<br>Be theme and hearing ever—was in this Britain<br>And conquered it, Cassibelan, thine uncle,<br>Famous in Caesar’s praises no whit less<br>Than in his feats deserving it, for him<br>And his succession granted Rome a tribute,<br>Yearly three thousand pounds, which by thee lately<br>Is left untendered. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | And, to kill the marvel,<br>Shall be so ever. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | There will be many Caesars<br>Ere such another Julius. Britain’s a world<br>By itself, and we will nothing pay<br>For wearing our own noses. |
| [[Queen\|QUEEN]] | That opportunity<br>Which then they had to take from ‘s, to resume<br>We have again. Remember, sir, my liege,<br>The kings your ancestors, together with<br>The natural bravery of your isle, which stands<br>As Neptune’s park, ribbed and paled in<br>With banks unscalable and roaring waters,<br>With sands that will not bear your enemies’ boats,<br>But suck them up to th’ topmast. A kind of conquest<br>Caesar made here, but made not here his brag<br>Of ‘came and saw and overcame’. With shame—<br>The first that ever touched him—he was carried<br>From off our coast, twice beaten; and his shipping,<br>Poor ignorant baubles, on our terrible seas<br>Like eggshells moved upon their surges, cracked<br>As easily ’gainst our rocks; for joy whereof<br>The famed Cassibelan, who was once at point—<br>O giglot fortune!—to master Caesar’s [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Made Lud’s town with rejoicing fires bright,<br>And Britons strut with [[Courage\|courage]]. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | Come, there’s no more tribute to be paid. Our kingdom is stronger than it was at that [[Time\|time]], and, as I said, there is no more such Caesars. Other of them may have crooked noses, but to owe such straight arms, none. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | Son, let your mother end. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | We have yet many among us can grip as hard as Cassibelan. I do not say I am one, but I have a hand. Why tribute? Why should we pay tribute? If Caesar can hide the sun from us with a blanket, or put the [[Moon\|moon]] in his pocket, we will pay him tribute for light; else, sir, no more tribute, pray you now. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | (to [[Lucius]]) You must know,<br>Till the injurious Romans did extort<br>This tribute from us we were free. Caesar’s [[Ambition\|ambition]],<br>Which swelled so much that it did almost stretch<br>The sides o‘th’ world, against all colour here<br>Did put the yoke upon ’s, which to shake off<br>Becomes a warlike people, whom we reckon<br>Ourselves to be. We do say then to Caesar,<br>Our ancestor was that Mulmutius which<br>Ordained our laws, whose use the [[Sword\|sword]] of Caesar<br>Hath too much mangled, whose repair and franchise<br>Shall by the power we hold be our good deed,<br>Though Rome be therefore angry. Mulmutius made<br>our laws,<br>Who was the first of Britain which did put<br>His brows within a golden [[Crown\|crown]] and called<br>Himself a [[King\|king]]. |
| [[Lucius\|LUCIUS]] | I am sorry, [[Cymbeline]],<br>That I am to pronounce Augustus Caesar—<br>Caesar, that hath more kings his servants than<br>Thyself domestic officers—thine enemy.<br>Receive it from me, then: war and confusion<br>In Caesar’s name pronounce I ’gainst thee. Look<br>For fury not to be resisted. Thus defied,<br>I thank thee for myself. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | Thou art welcome, Caius.<br>Thy Caesar knighted me; my youth I spent<br>Much under him; of him I gathered [[Honour\|honour]],<br>Which he to seek of me again perforce<br>Behoves me keep at utterance. I am perfect<br>That the Pannonians and Dalmatians for<br>Their liberties are now in arms, a precedent<br>Which not to read would show the Britons cold;<br>So Caesar shall not find them. |
| [[Lucius\|LUCIUS]] | Let proof speak. |
| [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]] | His majesty bids you welcome. Make pastime with us a day or two or longer. If you seek us afterwards in other terms, you shall find us in our salt-[[Water\|water]] girdle. If you beat us out of it, it is yours; if you fall in the adventure, our crows shall fare the better for you; and there’s an end. |
| [[Lucius\|LUCIUS]] | So, sir. |
| [[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]] | I know your master’s pleasure, and he mine.<br>All the remain is ‘Welcome’. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Pisanio, reading of a letter |
| PISANIO | How? Of adultery? Wherefore write you not<br>What [[Monster\|monster]]’s her accuser? Leonatus,<br>O master, what a strange infection<br>Is fall’n into thy ear! What false Italian,<br>As poisonous tongued as handed, hath prevailed<br>On thy too ready hearing? Disloyal? No.<br>She’s punished for her [[Truth\|truth]], and undergoes,<br>More goddess-like than wife-like, such assaults<br>As would take in some virtue. O my master,<br>Thy mind to hers is now as low as were<br>Thy fortunes. How? That I should murder her,<br>Upon the [[Love\|love]] and [[Truth\|truth]] and vows which I<br>Have made to thy command? I her? Her [[Blood\|blood]]?<br>If it be so to do good service, never<br>Let me be counted serviceable. How look I,<br>That I should seem to lack humanity<br>So much as this fact comes to? (Reads) ‘Do’t. The letter<br>That I have sent her, by her own command<br>Shall give thee opportunity.’ O damned paper,<br>Black as the ink that’s on thee! Senseless bauble,<br>Art thou a fedary for this act, and look’st<br>So virgin-like without? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Innogen]] |
| PISANIO | Lo, here she comes.<br>I am ignorant in what I am commanded. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | How now, Pisanio? |
| PISANIO | Madam, here is a letter from my lord. |
| [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]] | Who, thy lord that is my lord, Leonatus?<br>O learned indeed were that astronomer<br>That knew the [[Stars\|stars]] as I his characters—<br>He’d lay the future open. You good gods,<br>Let what is here contained relish of [[Love\|love]],<br>Of my lord’s health, of his content—yet not<br>That we two are asunder; let that grieve him.<br>Some griefs are med’cinable; that is one of them,<br>For it doth physic [[Love\|love]]—of his content<br>All but in that. Good wax, thy leave. Blest be<br>You [[Bees\|bees]] that make these locks of counsel! [[lovers\|Lovers]]<br>And men in dangerous bonds pray not alike;<br>Though forfeiters you cast in [[Prison\|prison]], yet<br>You clasp young Cupid’s tables. Good news, gods!<br>She opens and reads the letter<br>’Justice and your father’s wrath, should he take me in<br>his dominion, could not be so cruel to me as you, O<br>the dearest of creatures, would even renew me with<br>your eyes. Take notice that I am in Cambria, at Milford<br>Haven. What your own [[Love\|love]] will out of this advise you,<br>follow. So he wishes you all happiness, that remains<br>loyal to his vow, and your increasing in [[Love\|love]],<br>Leonatus [[Posthumus]].’<br>O for a [[Horse\|horse]] with wings! Hear‘st thou, Pisanio?<br>He is at Milford Haven. Read, and tell me<br>How far ’tis thither. If one of mean affairs<br>May plod it in a week, why may not I<br>Glide thither in a day? Then, true Pisanio,<br>Who long‘st like me to see thy lord, who long’st—<br>O let me bate—but not like me—yet long‘st<br>But in a fainter kind—O, not like me,<br>For mine’s beyond beyond; say, and speak thick—<br>[[Love]]’s counsellor should fill the bores of hearing,<br>To th’ smothering of the sense—how far it is<br>To this same blessèd Milford. And by th’ way<br>Tell me how Wales was made so happy as<br>T’inherit such a haven. But first of all,<br>How we may steal from hence; and for the gap<br>That we shall make in [[Time\|time]] from our hence-going<br>Till our return, to excuse; but first, how get hence.<br>Why should excuse be born or ere begot?<br>We’ll talk of that hereafter.
